One entry per working session, added at the end of the session. Entries are kept as written; the order is as recorded (newest first since 2026-11-05, older entries below in date order).

- **2026-11-14 — First full CI run since re-enable (`fix/ci-first-run-failures`); 0.3.0 cut (`chore/release-0.3.0`).** Run 43556607235 was red on all three OSes. Fixes: `ci.yml` sets `PINNED_BAYAN_REF: HEAD` for every step of the job (a depth-1 checkout has no `dev`, so `SourcesAreFormatted` failed on Linux) and a `concurrency` group with `cancel-in-progress`; the T9 fake compiler is `zig.exe` on Windows (`Toolchain.FindOnPath` takes only `.exe`/`.cmd`/`.bat` there; wp1 94/94). Linux T18 x1–x3 (`member.probes.-.x1..x3`: the self-hosted side bound `other.oc` as `errorExpr`) was a race, not a compiler fault: `SelfhostDriverT1Tests.SourcesFormatCheckAsCliApp`, in a parallel class, called `BootstrapTests.Stage2Executable()`, whose first use builds the stages with `Environment.CurrentDirectory = Golden.RepoRoot`, so T18's relative `tmp/t18-cases` pointed elsewhere for that window. The theory moved to `SelfhostDriverStage2FormatTests` in the serial `ToolchainEnvironment` collection; break-test: the old layout run with T18 failed x1–x3 locally (3 of 127), the new one passes 128 of 128. Rule: every caller of `Stage2Executable` runs in `ToolchainEnvironment`. Release: `cut.sh 0.3.0` after the example `-dev` strings were made neutral; `bootstrap/bayan.c` regenerated (version strings only); CHANGELOG summary and known limits written; `docs/check.sh`, `package.test.sh`, `memory-gate.test.sh` and `memory-gate.sh` pass. Stage-2 build of `selfhost/driver` on macOS: 1.69 GB median of 3 (was 2.94 GB on 2026-11-12), now the known-limit figure in `package.sh`, `RELEASING.md` and `memory-leaks.md`. Pre-push full suites on `test/release-0.3.0-preflight` (b382b246, release + this fix): VM (bayan-test01, 2 shards) 13,693 passed, 0 failed, 31.8 min; wp1 (Windows 11) 13,685 passed, 0 failed, 1 skipped, 45 min (the known emitter speed failure did not occur). The release commit first held a stray 3.3 MB `driver` binary (the stage-2 measurement build writes `./driver` without `-o`); rebuilt without it before any push.

- **2026-11-13 — vm2 crash root cause; arena per mutant re-bind (plan 012, `fix/mutate-arena-per-rebind`).** The vm2 MSB4166 crashes were the kernel OOM killer: the T10 harness (self-hosted `mutate` over every run input) reached 4.4 GB in the 11 GiB VM beside a second slot, and systemd's default `OOMPolicy=stop` then stopped the whole unit, `dotnet test` with it. Three fixes: `shard-test.sh` units run with `OOMPolicy=continue` (`fix/shard-vm-node-reuse`, merged before this branch) (an OOM kill now fails one test; the two-slot run on that branch finished, 13,599 passed, the 2 failures were the two std-json `mutate` runs, exit 137); the T10 all-inputs tests run 32 inputs per process; and the cause: `schemataRebinds` re-compiled the whole program per site under `General`, which never frees (Q31). Each site's re-bind (and `schemataMarkOf`'s source copy) now runs in its own arena: `std-json.byn` 2,490 MB → 23 MB, all run inputs in one process 5,541 MB → 179 MB, output byte-identical. New `SelfhostDefaultT10Tests.MutateOfALargeInputStaysBounded` (break-tested: 2,349 MB sampled on the old code); the peak sampler is shared (`PeakSampler`). Open: the bootstrap and `doctor` peaks (`work/todo.md`).

- **2026-11-13 — Plan 009: prebuilt runtime object (`docs/plans/2026-11-13-009-prebuilt-runtime-object.md`).** `Toolchain.cs` compiles `bayan-rt.c` once per toolchain, compiler identity and flag set into `cache/v1/runtime/<key>/bayan-rt.o` (checksum checked on lookup, a damaged object is rebuilt) and links it; the program key includes the C compiler's identity (`CompilerIdentity`: `--version` or `zig version` output plus `CompilerStamp`, the resolved compiler file's path and last write time, with no hash and no size since the driver has no size builtin (Q1148); the command and the stamp when the compiler can't report its version; memoised up to 64 per process, probe timeout min(30 s, compile timeout)); `Evict` and `clean --cache` cover `runtime/`. Ported line by line to selfhost `cache.byn` (`t9RuntimeObject`, `t9LookupRuntime`, `t9CompilerIdentity`) and `cc.byn` (`ccRuntimeArguments`). A script pointer (`cache/v1/scripts/<key>`) holds the program key and, on its second line, the stamp of the compiler found when it was written; `LookupScript` misses only when a compiler is found now with another stamp, so a remembered script still starts with no C compiler installed, and a pointer from before the stamp misses once and is rewritten. `ScriptKey` is unchanged. Tests: `CacheTests` (runtime once per flag set, damaged object, identity change, write-time change, script pointer after a compiler change, old-format pointer, ASan/cross variants, eviction, stats/clean, parallel race) and `SelfhostDefaultT9Tests` `*AsToolchain` parity (shared runtime, damaged object, identity change, write-time change, script pointer, old-format pointer, a cross build's own runtime object (skipped unless the compiler is zig), eviction, clean) and `ParallelBuildsNeverCorruptTheCache`, which counts `runtime/` entries as the C# one does; ASan parity is C#-only (the driver has no sanitize builds); each break-tested. A warm stage-2 build of hello with the real zig takes 0.19 s (1.64 s with the earlier hash), a cold one 1.51 s. Deviations: the stamp is the path as found (not resolved through links) with the write time of the final link target, as stat(2) reads it; the driver ignores `BAYAN_CC` (as before); the identity memo is per process and per command.

L005 is proven by `L005RunBatchTests` (33 edge cases plus 4,000 generated through `byn_batch_cmdline` and a `cmd.exe` read-back model; off Windows, refused and accepted calls return `unsupported`). - **2026-10-21 — Spec 003 (ownership verification).** `x.take()` (BYN2788), `switch (move x)` with owning bindings (BYN2789; BYN2790 for two bindings whose full paths overlap and both own storage; BYN2791 for a `when` guard that mutates or moves its arm's binding, Q70), a debug-build runtime ownership checker, and a seeded ownership program generator (with `when` guards). L008 enacted with its proof; L006 amended (43 rejected, 26 accepted rows). Checker details:
  - A `switch (move x)` binding is claimed by a lowered `LClaim` at the start of the selected arm.
  - `Operands` spills a `move` that a later operand reads.
  - Owned places passed by `ref`/`out` or as a method receiver claim their storage after the call. Element receivers are not tracked.
  - `byn_own_rekey` releases the target before the lookup; `byn_own_remove` clears the slot.
  - The checker is compiled into debug builds only: the toolchain passes `-DBYN_NDEBUG` for safe and fast, debug `--emit-c` defines `BYN_DEBUG`, and `bayan-rt.c` defaults to the checker when neither is set. `SafeAndFastExecutablesHaveNoChecker` asserts no `byn_own_` symbol in safe/fast executables.
  - The ASan probe honours `BAYAN_CC`, with compile/run timeouts of 120 s and 10 s.

  The review ran three two-lens loops with refutation; every confirmed finding was fixed. Going past the 3-loop cap of `/code-review` was deliberate: loop 3 found a double free in safe mode (guard mutation plus `realloc`), and the conservative fix (BYN2791) came from AGENTS.md's spec-gap rule. Safe/fast `--emit-c` of every `run/` golden is byte-identical to dev (116 files). 829 tests pass; all 8 laws hold. Open: `work/todo.md` items F (`StrBuf.take()` refill context), the `_ = move a` help text, and a claim skipped for a root moved on a branch that does not run. Worker spend: $98.22 (writers $29.79, gates $45.20, think $7.51, reviews $12.57, audits $3.15). **Next:** spec 004 (port friction), already in progress on `feat/004-port-friction`.
- **2026-10-21 — Spec 004 (port friction).** Every item of the lexer port's friction list that the road assigns to this spec (R1–R12):
  - Arguments named like their parameter (`start`, `tok.start`) count as named for BYN2305; implied names in a swapped order are BYN2307 (Q72).
  - The twelve declaration-only keywords are contextual in the parser, and `law` left the reserved range in both lexers (Q73; L001 holds).
  - An unannotated `var` integer literal takes the type of its first constraining use, by a two-pass bind (spec §2.3, Q74).
  - `bayan types` prints the typed dump (`docs/typed-dump.md`, `tests/cases/typed/`, Q76).
  - A method returning a span may lend its own fields, and a mutating method call mutates the receiver's root (spec §9.4, Q75, Q78).
  - `string.from` / `string.tryFrom` with `Utf8Error` (Q71; the separate offset function dropped by revision 0004).
  - `catch { … }` and `catch (e) { … }` blocks, BYN2611 when one on a value-returning call can complete normally (Q77).
  - `const` string arrays (BYN2229 reworded), `List.removeRange`, integer `toHex()`.

  L009 (typed dump deterministic) and L010 (decode accepts exactly valid UTF-8) enacted with their proofs. Spec §2.3, §2.4, §3.2, §3.5, §8, §9.4 and §10 updated. One review finding was refuted. 979 tests pass. Open in `work/todo.md`: R4 gaps (group order dependence, a group mixing a narrow integer and a float use), an untyped array literal as a `for` source, and a load-sensitive allocation test. Q79–Q86 answered, Q87 recorded. New codes BYN2709 (value not an `i32` integer literal), BYN2710 (value on a case with data), BYN2711 (two cases share a value, implicit ones included), BYN2712 (warning: `_` arm over a union declared in this package; help lists the uncovered cases). An untyped `[1, 2, 3]` is `i32[3]`. Spreads `..xs` (`BoundSpread`) go into `List<T>` (a copy loop), and into `T[N]`/`Span<T>` (stack storage) only from fixed arrays, since the length must be known; a list or span spread there is BYN2219 (existing code, no new one). New BYN2235 `SpreadNotSequence`, BYN2236 `MapLiteralMixed`. Goldens `run/collection-literals`, `parse/collection-literals`, `diag/spread-not-sequence`, `diag/map-literal-mixed`, `diag/spread-run-time-length`; `CollectionLiteralTests` checks span literals emit a stack array. `Set<T>` literals and the `length`/`add` renames (3.4) are not done.

Diagnostics BYN2800–BYN2816. Law L012 (static and dynamic dispatch agree) enacted with its proof (compiled with `-Wall -Werror`, Q102). Q89–Q113 recorded, Q88 unused. - **2026-10-23 — Spec 006 re-review fixes (C–G) and merge.** Region analysis now tracks borrows of `this` fields, `dyn ref` for-variables, element writes and by-value element arguments (C1–C4). It also tracks borrows of `ref`/`out`/`in` arguments by calls returning a span or `dyn ref`, a `ref this` call that structurally changes a borrowed field (a bodiless callee changes every field), `ref items` while borrowed, loops over a `this` List field, and span `dyn ref` loops (E1–E5). `for dyn ref` mutates element slots. A `unique` field cannot witness a getter (BYN2780), and a `unique` `Item` under a constraint is reported at instantiation (D1–D3). A `for dyn ref` loop that mutates elements is a write to the array: it is BYN2104 on `let`, and BYN2311 no longer fires on `var` (F). Before this fix, the `let` form compiled and mutated the array. `move` and `.take()` while a span or `dyn ref` still borrows the value is the new error BYN3006 "moved while borrowed" (G1, the review's B5 hole). A borrow's loan now starts where the expression first lends the owner, not at the end of the initializer, so `first(w.items.asSpan(), w.grow())` is BYN3004 (G2). Spec R2 states the field-witness rule as built. A generic stand-in audit found region analysis sound for instantiated generics; uninstantiated generic structs are not region-checked (`work/todo.md`). The cross-check of the 007–012 specs against 006 as built is applied on the feat/007, feat/008 and feat/009 branches, all unmerged. A final review of the fixes (d5801b7..14dff73, `low`, three loops) found 6, then 4, then 4 more, all confirmed by probe, none refuted. They are fixed as H, I and J. H: `ref this` arguments change every field; `for dyn ref` over a `ReadOnlySpan` is BYN2106; changed fields are memoized per method (Tarjan SCCs on `Compilation`), so 200 `ref this` methods check in 0.17s instead of 18.4s; ref arguments lend only when their type can hold storage; the loan starts at the stored borrow; a writable `for dyn ref` over a temporary is accepted again. I: place-ness by shape; a `dyn ref` result keeps its ref arguments' regions (BYN3002); writable `dyn ref` arguments count as writes. J: span-index, `with`, `try` and `catch` places; `this` outlives the method body; passing or calling through a writable `dyn ref` value mutates its owners. Open, in `work/todo.md`: `asSpan()` on a `let` (Q114), and two narrower `dyn ref` tracking gaps. Full suite 1587/1587 after J (one unnamed failure in two of five full runs under load, gone on rerun; `LexerTests.OneMegabyteAllocatesOnlyTheTokenArray` is the known load flake). All 12 laws hold. The C–J break-tests fail as expected. Spend: C $2.51, D $6.81, E gates $2.14 + $2.33, F $0.24 + gates $3.50 + $2.01, G $1.73 + gates $4.60 + $1.82, spec cross-check $2.43, 009 doc writer gates $2.52 + $1.92, generic audit $0.62, final review loops $0.88 + $0.93 + $1.33, H $6.50 gate, I $0.92 + $1.10 gate, J $1.90 + $7.80 gate.

T10` writes `tmp/010-t10/prompt.md`, a self-contained writer brief (task text, plan rows, the task's `010-api.md` lines, harness use, writer protocol, Bayan cheat sheet, guard command; `BAYAN_BASE=<branch>` sets the guard's base). A task is done only when `bash scripts/selfhost/guard.sh …` (the brief's line) prints `GUARD PASS`: scope, no junk files, 0 build warnings, filtered tests green with none skipped, no tuple destructuring/`.N`, and N mutations of the main `.byn` each killed by a test. Then review against the task's done-when, merge. Parallel tasks conflict only in the Questions list: keep both sides in number order. **Lessons:** a writer brief must say restore break-tests by copying the file back (a `git checkout --` reverts to the stub and loses the port); resuming a long writer for a few wrap-up steps costs $4–6 in context reload, so start small finishing runs fresh; ~80 turns is the practical size of one task; run at most 2 writers with `nice` on a shared machine (four parallel writers pushed the load average past 30 on 10 cores). **Needs decision (user):** DeepSeek top-up; what to do when the Claude subscription runs out (DeepSeek writers + unattended loop, pause, or per-token Claude); L017 wording vs fixing Q505's backtick wrapping; enacting L018 in a narrowed form; continuing into 011–012 on other models.

- **2026-11-03 — Parser fuzzing: three C# crashes fixed, selfhost port has the same bugs (branch `fix/parser-fuzz-crashes`, stacked on `test/runtime-properties`, not merged).** A byte-level fuzzer (corpus splices from `tests/cases/**/*.byn`, grammar fragments, random bytes; 210k inputs) checked lexer invariants (ordered in-bounds tokens on code-point boundaries, one EOF at the end, trivia flags match the gaps, diagnostic and fix spans in bounds, determinism, applying a fix lowers its code's count) and that the parser never throws. The lexer held. The parser threw on three inputs, now fixed with cases in `ParserTests.TruncatedInputReportsWithoutThrowing`: `int()fails` (`ExpectName` read `_tokens[-1]` for a C# primitive name as the first token), `if(/*` (`ParseBody` built a negative span when the braceless body took no token at the end of the file), and `unless ` + 19 × `(` + `)` (`IsParenthesizedCondition` read `_tokens[_count]` when the token buffer was exactly full; it now clamps as line 676 does). Full suite: 7067/7075; 4 failures are environment (no zig: Doctor, ZigVersionPin, two OutputTests; FormatTests' unreadable file runs as root). The other 3 are expected: `SelfhostParserNoPanicLaw`, `SelfhostParserEquivalenceLaw` and `SelfhostSemanticsNoPanicLaw` used to replace inputs that crash the C# parser with empty ones; those inputs now reach the port, which panics at `selfhost/lexer/source.byn:80` (`getText` of the negative span, from `reportBraceless` in `selfhost/parser/stmts.byn:415`/`stmts2.byn:140`). The port also has the `kindAt(p.pos - 2)` read (`recover.byn:152`) and needs the same three fixes through `land.sh` (Q712) before this branch merges.

- **2026-11-06 — fix/runtime-security: runtime audit fixes.** From `docs/security/2026-11-06-runtime-audit.md`. `byn_arena_take` panics "arena allocation is too large" before rounding when `n < 0` or `n > INT64_MAX - 15 - sizeof(byn_chunk)`, and its room and doubling checks cannot overflow (`run/memory-arena-repeated-too-large`, `run/memory-arena-padded-too-large`, both compilers). Map and `Symbol` hashing is SipHash-1-3 keyed by 128 bits (`byn_hash_seed`, `byn_hash_seed_hi`) from `arc4random_buf` (macOS/BSD), `getrandom` (Linux) or `BCryptGenRandom` (Windows, looked up from System32 `bcrypt.dll` with `LoadLibraryExW`/`GetProcAddress` so no link flag changes); the key is zero under `--test`; `byn_hash_mix` is SipHash over the 16 bytes of both parts; no golden output changed; `SipHashTests` checks Rust's `test_siphash_1_3` vectors. `byn_slice_range` rejects `^n` and `^end` outside `0..len` and an inclusive end of `i64.max` before computing (`run/slice-from-end-min`, `run/slice-inclusive-max`; the goldens passed before the fix too, since `-fwrapv` wrapped into the slice check, so the break-test was UBSan on the old header: two signed-overflow reports, none after). `replaced`, `joined` and `byn_list_reserve` use `__builtin_*_overflow` and panic "... is too long" (`RuntimeSizeOverflowTests`, a C harness). `libc-uses.txt` drops `getpid`/`GetCurrentProcessId`, adds `GetProcAddress`, `LoadLibraryExW`, `arc4random_buf`, `getrandom`; `011-c-inventory.md` gains rows for the five new `__builtin_*_overflow` calls. Stdin is done (user, 2026-11-06 and 2026-11-07): `Io.stdin.readAll()` fails with `invalidUtf8` on bytes that are not UTF-8 (`byn_io_read_stdin` checks `byn_utf8_valid`; `run/io-stdin-invalid-utf8`), and the new `Io.stdin.readAllBytes()` returns `Result<List<u8>, IoError>` unchecked (`byn_io_read_stdin_bytes`; `run/io-stdin-bytes`), which the selfhost lexer, parser and `bayan lex -` now read; `BYN_RUNTIME_VERSION` is 2. Out of scope items are `work/todo.md` lines.
